 \e[34m\e[1m--tmp-env-file          \e[0m  Temporary environment file(s) to load

                               deleted after loading. [=@[]].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--tmp-env0-file         \e[0m  Temporary environment file(s) to load

 \e[34m\e[1m--inspect               \e[0m  Node.js inspector port (injected by Playwright).\e[0m

The following options are available:

                               prefix.\e[0m

 \e[34m\e[1m--thread-sync-order     \e[0m  Thread synchronization order the replay enforces
                               on a Windows native recording: 'total' or
                               'per-object' (env CT_THREAD_SYNC_ORDER; the flag
                               wins). Unset: the replay backend's default.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--os-simulation         \e[0m  Replay mode for a native recording:
                               'weak-userspace', 'strong-userspace' or
                               'strong-kernel' (env CT_OS_SIMULATION; the flag
                               wins). Unset: the replay backend's default.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--follow-request-spans  \e[0m  Follow requests in a growing container: '1' or
                               '0' (env CT_FOLLOW_REQUEST_SPANS; the flag wins).
                               Default off.\e[0m

The following options are available:

 \e[34m\e[1m-t, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--trace-folder          \e[0m  the trace output folder.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-i, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--interactive           \e[0m  explicit flag for interactively choosing a trace.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--org                   \e[0m  organization to upload to.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--token                 \e[0m  bearer token (uses stored token if omitted).\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--base-url              \e[0m  override the remote server URL.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--no-portable           \e[0m  skip adding portable binaries/symbols to MCR
                               traces before upload.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--no-split-upload       \e[0m  skip pre-split slice detection and force full
                               trace upload even when slices are present.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--omniscient-db         \e[0m  how the server prepares the omniscient-DB
                               artefacts for the uploaded slice (off | on | lazy
                               | pre-prepared). See spec §6.8.6 / M31.
                               [=OmniscientDbMode.off].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--artifact              \e[0m  path of a local artifact to share (a recording
                               folder, or a review dataset directory produced by
                               `ct review collect`).\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--kind                  \e[0m  what --artifact holds (recording |
                               review-dataset); only needed when it cannot be
                               recognised from the path itself.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--encrypt               \e[0m  encrypt the payload on this computer before
                               uploading. The password is never sent to the
                               service, and a lost password cannot be recovered
                               — not by you and not by CodeTracer.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--password-file         \e[0m  read the encryption password from this file
                               instead of prompting. Passing a password as a
                               command-line argument is not supported: it would
                               be visible to every process on the machine.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--password-stdin        \e[0m  read the encryption password from standard input
                               (for scripts and CI). Mutually exclusive with
                               --password-file.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--visibility            \e[0m  who may read the stored artifact: 'tenant'
                               (members of the owning organisation, the default)
                               or 'tenant-or-invite' (those, plus anyone holding
                               an invite link).\e[0m

\e[36mct record\e[0m [OPTIONS]... <recordProgram>\e[0m <recordArgs>\e[0m

\e[34m\e[1m <recordProgram>\e[0m              \e[0m  Program to record.\e[0m
\e[34m\e[1m <recordArgs>\e[0m                 \e[0m  Arguments for the recorded program [=@[]].\e[0m
                             \e[0m  Everything after `--` is passed to the recorded
                               program unchanged, including flags..\e[0m

The following options are available:

 \e[34m\e[1m--lang                  \e[0m  Language of the recording (auto-detected from the
                               program path)..\e[0m
                             \e[0m  Leave blank to auto-detect. Python scripts use
                               the db backend and run with the same interpreter
                               you would get from `python`, honoring
                               CODETRACER_PYTHON_INTERPRETER, PYTHON_EXECUTABLE,
                               PYTHONEXECUTABLE, PYTHON, or PATH. Ensure that
                               interpreter has the codetracer_python_recorder
                               package installed..\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-o, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--output-folder         \e[0m  Output folder for the recording [=.].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--backend               \e[0m  Native recording backend. MCR is the default on
                               every host, and `mcr` also pins it. Linux
                               additionally accepts `rr`; Windows accepts `ttd`.
                               A value this host cannot honour is refused, never
                               silently downgraded. Not a language selector: see
                               --lang..\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-e, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--export                \e[0m  Export zip file for the recording.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-t, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--stylus-trace          \e[0m  Path to a stylus emv trace json file.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-a, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--address               \e[0m  Address when we are recording in ci
                               mode/environment.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--socket                \e[0m  Path to socket for sending the trace events
                               metadata when in ci mode/environment.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--with-diff             \e[0m  Record a diff related to this trace and produce a
                               multitrace. Arg can be `last-commit`, path to a
                               diff file (must be from the current repo!) or a
                               valid `git diff <arg>` arg.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--store-trace-folder-for-pid\e[0m  sets a pid, if we should store the resulting
                               trace folder in a special tmp file, grouping info
                               for a certain originating codetracer pid. 0 means
                               'do not store in such a file' [=0].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--upload                \e[0m  upload the trace directly after recording and
                               processing it.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--use-interpose         \e[0m  Record graphics API calls for visual replay (MCR
                               backend only). [=false].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--server                \e[0m  Record a long-lived server. The recording stays
                               readable while it runs, so `ct replay -t
                               <folder>` in another terminal shows requests
                               arriving live; stop it with Ctrl-C. [=false].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--portable              \e[0m  Make the trace replayable on another machine, or
                               later after its files change: the MCR backend
                               bundles every file the program mapped, their
                               debug symbols and the platform description into
                               the trace, and the rr backend packs the files the
                               trace mapped into it. A backend that cannot do
                               this yet refuses the flag. Also honors
                               CODETRACER_PORTABLE=on|off; --upload implies it,
                               and warns where it is not implemented. [=false].\e[0m

                               readable while it runs, so `ct replay -t
                               <folder>` in another terminal shows requests
                               arriving live; stop it with Ctrl-C. [=false].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--portable              \e[0m  Make the trace replayable on another machine, or
                               later after its files change: the MCR backend
                               bundles every file the program mapped, their
                               debug symbols and the platform description into
                               the trace, and the rr backend packs the files the
                               trace mapped into it. A backend that cannot do
                               this yet refuses the flag. Also honors
                               CODETRACER_PORTABLE=on|off; --upload implies it,
                               and warns where it is not implemented. [=false].\e[0m
\e[1m
\e[36mct record-test\e[0m [OPTIONS]...

The following options are available:

 \e[34m\e[1m--with-diff             \e[0m  Record a diff related to this trace and produce a
                               multitrace. Arg can be `last-commit`, path to a
                               diff file (must be from the current repo!) or a
                               valid `git diff <arg>` arg.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--store-trace-folder-for-pid\e[0m  sets a pid, if we should store the resulting
                               trace folder in a special tmp file, grouping info
                               for a certain originating codetracer pid. 0 means
                               'do not store in such a file' [=0].\e[0m

                               program unchanged, including flags..\e[0m

The following options are available:

 \e[34m\e[1m--lang                  \e[0m  Language of the recording (auto-detected from the
                               program path)..\e[0m
                             \e[0m  Leave blank to auto-detect. Python scripts use
                               the db backend and run with the same interpreter
                               you would get from `python`, honoring
                               CODETRACER_PYTHON_INTERPRETER, PYTHON_EXECUTABLE,
                               PYTHONEXECUTABLE, PYTHON, or PATH. Ensure that
                               interpreter has the codetracer_python_recorder
                               package installed..\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-o, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--output-folder         \e[0m  Output folder for the recording [=.].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--backend               \e[0m  Native recording backend. MCR is the default on
                               every host, and `mcr` also pins it. Linux
                               additionally accepts `rr`; Windows accepts `ttd`.
                               A value this host cannot honour is refused, never
                               silently downgraded. Not a language selector: see
                               --lang..\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-e, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--export                \e[0m  Export zip file for the recording.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-t, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--stylus-trace          \e[0m  Path to a stylus emv trace json file.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-a, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--address               \e[0m  Address when we are recording in ci
                               mode/environment.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--socket                \e[0m  Path to socket for sending the trace events
                               metadata when in ci mode/environment.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--with-diff             \e[0m  Record a diff related to this trace and produce a
                               multitrace. Arg can be `last-commit`, path to a
                               diff file (must be from the current repo!) or a
                               valid `git diff <arg>` arg.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--store-trace-folder-for-pid\e[0m  sets a pid, if we should store the resulting
                               trace folder in a special tmp file, grouping info
                               for a certain originating codetracer pid. 0 means
                               'do not store in such a file' [=0].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--upload                \e[0m  upload the trace directly after recording and
                               processing it.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--use-interpose         \e[0m  Record graphics API calls for visual replay (MCR
                               backend only). [=false].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--server                \e[0m  Record a long-lived server. The recording stays
                               readable while it runs, so `ct replay -t
                               <folder>` in another terminal shows requests
                               arriving live; stop it with Ctrl-C. [=false].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--portable              \e[0m  Make the trace replayable on another machine, or
                               later after its files change: the MCR backend
                               bundles every file the program mapped, their
                               debug symbols and the platform description into
                               the trace, and the rr backend packs the files the
                               trace mapped into it. A backend that cannot do
                               this yet refuses the flag. Also honors
                               CODETRACER_PORTABLE=on|off; --upload implies it,
                               and warns where it is not implemented. [=false].\e[0m

                               on a Windows native recording: 'total' or
                               'per-object' (env CT_THREAD_SYNC_ORDER; the flag
                               wins). Unset: the replay backend's default.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--os-simulation         \e[0m  Replay mode for a native recording:
                               'weak-userspace', 'strong-userspace' or

                               wins). Unset: the replay backend's default.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--follow-request-spans  \e[0m  Follow requests in a growing container: '1' or

                               Default off.\e[0m

                               folder, or a review dataset directory produced by
                               `ct review collect`).\e[0m

                               review-dataset); only needed when it cannot be
                               recognised from the path itself.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--encrypt               \e[0m  encrypt the payload on this computer before
                               uploading. The password is never sent to the
                               service, and a lost password cannot be recovered
                               — not by you and not by CodeTracer.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--password-file         \e[0m  read the encryption password from this file
                               instead of prompting. Passing a password as a
                               command-line argument is not supported: it would
                               be visible to every process on the machine.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--password-stdin        \e[0m  read the encryption password from standard input
                               (for scripts and CI). Mutually exclusive with
                               --password-file.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--visibility            \e[0m  who may read the stored artifact: 'tenant'
                               (members of the owning organisation, the default)
                               or 'tenant-or-invite' (those, plus anyone holding
                               an invite link).\e[0m

 \e[34m\e[1m--lang                  \e[0m  Language of the recording (auto-detected from the
                               program path)..\e[0m
                             \e[0m  Leave blank to auto-detect. Python scripts use
                               the db backend and run with the same interpreter
                               you would get from `python`, honoring
                               CODETRACER_PYTHON_INTERPRETER, PYTHON_EXECUTABLE,
                               PYTHONEXECUTABLE, PYTHON, or PATH. Ensure that
                               interpreter has the codetracer_python_recorder
                               package installed..\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-o, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--output-folder         \e[0m  Output folder for the recording [=.].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--backend               \e[0m  Native recording backend. MCR is the default on
                               every host, and `mcr` also pins it. Linux
                               additionally accepts `rr`; Windows accepts `ttd`.
                               A value this host cannot honour is refused, never
                               silently downgraded. Not a language selector: see
                               --lang..\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-e, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--export                \e[0m  Export zip file for the recording.\e[0m

                               metadata when in ci mode/environment.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--with-diff             \e[0m  Record a diff related to this trace and produce a
                               multitrace. Arg can be `last-commit`, path to a
                               diff file (must be from the current repo!) or a
                               valid `git diff <arg>` arg.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--store-trace-folder-for-pid\e[0m  sets a pid, if we should store the resulting
                               trace folder in a special tmp file, grouping info
                               for a certain originating codetracer pid. 0 means
                               'do not store in such a file' [=0].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--upload                \e[0m  upload the trace directly after recording and
                               processing it.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--use-interpose         \e[0m  Record graphics API calls for visual replay (MCR
                               backend only). [=false].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--server                \e[0m  Record a long-lived server. The recording stays
                               readable while it runs, so `ct replay -t
                               <folder>` in another terminal shows requests
                               arriving live; stop it with Ctrl-C. [=false].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--portable              \e[0m  Make the trace replayable on another machine, or
                               later after its files change: the MCR backend
                               bundles every file the program mapped, their
                               debug symbols and the platform description into
                               the trace, and the rr backend packs the files the
                               trace mapped into it. A backend that cannot do
                               this yet refuses the flag. Also honors
                               CODETRACER_PORTABLE=on|off; --upload implies it,
                               and warns where it is not implemented. [=false].\e[0m

 \e[34m\e[1m--server                \e[0m
 Record a long-lived server. The
 recording stays readable while it
 runs, so `ct replay -t <folder>` in
 another terminal shows requests
 arriving live; stop it with Ctrl-C.
 [=false].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--portable              \e[0m
 Make the trace replayable on another
 machine, or later after its files
 change: the MCR backend bundles every
 file the program mapped, their debug
 symbols and the platform description
 into the trace, and the rr backend
 packs the files the trace mapped into
 it. A backend that cannot do this yet
 refuses the flag. Also honors
 CODETRACER_PORTABLE=on|off; --upload
 implies it, and warns where it is not
 implemented. [=false].\e[0m
\e[1m
\e[36mct record-test\e[0m [OPTIONS]...

The following options are available:

 \e[34m\e[1m--with-diff             \e[0m
 Record a diff related to this trace
 and produce a multitrace. Arg can be
 `last-commit`, path to a diff file
 (must be from the current repo!) or a
 valid `git diff <arg>` arg.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--store-trace-folder-for-pid\e[0m
 sets a pid, if we should store the
 resulting trace folder in a special
 tmp file, grouping info for a certain
 originating codetracer pid. 0 means
 'do not store in such a file' [=0].\e[0m

                               replay -t <folder>` in another terminal shows requests arriving live; stop it
                               with Ctrl-C. [=false].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--portable              \e[0m  Make the trace replayable on another machine, or later after its files change:
                               the MCR backend bundles every file the program mapped, their debug symbols and
                               the platform description into the trace, and the rr backend packs the files the
                               trace mapped into it. A backend that cannot do this yet refuses the flag. Also
                               honors CODETRACER_PORTABLE=on|off; --upload implies it, and warns where it is
                               not implemented. [=false].\e[0m

 \e[34m\e[1m--password-file         \e[0m  read the encryption password from this file instead of prompting. Passing a

 \e[34m\e[1m--portable              \e[0m  Make the trace replayable on another machine, or later after its files change:

 \e[34m\e[1m--password-file         \e[0m  read the encryption password from this file

                               command-line argument is not supported: it would
                               be visible to every process on the machine.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--password-stdin        \e[0m  read the encryption password from standard input

                               --password-file.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--visibility            \e[0m  who may read the stored artifact: 'tenant'

                               an invite link).\e[0m

 \e[34m\e[1m--backend               \e[0m  Native recording backend. MCR is the default on
                               every host, and `mcr` also pins it. Linux
                               additionally accepts `rr`; Windows accepts `ttd`.
                               A value this host cannot honour is refused, never
                               silently downgraded. Not a language selector: see
                               --lang..\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-e, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--export                \e[0m  Export zip file for the recording.\e[0m

                               trace folder in a special tmp file, grouping info

                               readable while it runs, so `ct replay -t
                               <folder>` in another terminal shows requests
                               arriving live; stop it with Ctrl-C. [=false].\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m--portable              \e[0m  Make the trace replayable on another machine, or
                               later after its files change: the MCR backend
                               bundles every file the program mapped, their
                               debug symbols and the platform description into
                               the trace, and the rr backend packs the files the
                               trace mapped into it. A backend that cannot do
                               this yet refuses the flag. Also honors
                               CODETRACER_PORTABLE=on|off; --upload implies it,
                               and warns where it is not implemented. [=false].\e[0m

                            uploading. The password is never sent to the
                            service, and a lost password cannot be recovered —

                            of prompting. Passing a password as a command-line
                            argument is not supported: it would be visible to
                            every process on the machine.\e[0m

CodeTracer - the user-friendly time-travelling debugger

\e[0m\e[33mUsage:\x20
\e[0m\e[1m
\e[36mct record\e[0m [OPTIONS]... <recordProgram>\e[0m <recordArgs>\e[0m

\e[34m\e[1m <recordProgram>\e[0m              \e[0m  Program to record.\e[0m
\e[34m\e[1m <recordArgs>\e[0m                 \e[0m  Arguments for the recorded program [=@[]].\e[0m
                             \e[0m  Everything after `--` is passed to the recorded
                               program unchanged, including flags..\e[0m

The following options are available:

     \e[34m\e[1m--lang                  \e[0m  Language of the recording (auto-detected from the
                               program path)..\e[0m
                             \e[0m  Leave blank to auto-detect. Python scripts use
                               the db backend and run with the same interpreter
                               you would get from `python`, honoring
                               CODETRACER_PYTHON_INTERPRETER, PYTHON_EXECUTABLE,
                               PYTHONEXECUTABLE, PYTHON, or PATH. Ensure that
                               interpreter has the codetracer_python_recorder
                               package installed..\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-o, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--output-folder         \e[0m  Output folder for the recording [=.].\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--backend               \e[0m  Native recording backend. MCR is the default on
                               every host, and `mcr` also pins it. Linux
                               additionally accepts `rr`; Windows accepts `ttd`.
                               A value this host cannot honour is refused, never
                               silently downgraded. Not a language selector: see
                               --lang..\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-e, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--export                \e[0m  Export zip file for the recording.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-t, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--stylus-trace          \e[0m  Path to a stylus emv trace json file.\e[0m
 \e[34m\e[1m-a, \e[0m\e[34m\e[1m--address               \e[0m  Address when we are recording in ci
                               mode/environment.\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--socket                \e[0m  Path to socket for sending the trace events
                               metadata when in ci mode/environment.\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--with-diff             \e[0m  Record a diff related to this trace and produce a
                               multitrace. Arg can be `last-commit`, path to a
                               diff file (must be from the current repo!) or a
                               valid `git diff <arg>` arg.\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--store-trace-folder-for-pid\e[0m  sets a pid, if we should store the resulting
                               trace folder in a special tmp file, grouping info
                               for a certain originating codetracer pid. 0 means
                               'do not store in such a file' [=0].\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--upload                \e[0m  upload the trace directly after recording and
                               processing it.\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--use-interpose         \e[0m  Record graphics API calls for visual replay (MCR
                               backend only). [=false].\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--server                \e[0m  Record a long-lived server. The recording stays
                               readable while it runs, so `ct replay -t
                               <folder>` in another terminal shows requests
                               arriving live; stop it with Ctrl-C. [=false].\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--portable              \e[0m  Make the trace replayable on another machine, or
                               later after its files change: the MCR backend
                               bundles every file the program mapped, their
                               debug symbols and the platform description into
                               the trace, and the rr backend packs the files the
                               trace mapped into it. A backend that cannot do
                               this yet refuses the flag. Also honors
                               CODETRACER_PORTABLE=on|off; --upload implies it,
                               and warns where it is not implemented. [=false].\e[0m

### 47: review --help (intercepted)
argv: ct "review" "--help"
env: (none)
exit: 0
--- stdout
`ct review` — review a diff with its recorded executions (DeepReview).

Usage:
  ct review <PATH>                    open a review over an exported dataset
                                      (a review.json file, or a directory
                                      produced by `ct review collect`)
  ct review collect [OPTIONS]         produce a review dataset
  ct review inspect <PATH> [--format text|json]
                                      summarise a dataset without a GUI

`ct review collect` options:
  --repo <DIR>          git repository the diff is read from
  --diff <BASE..HEAD>   diff specification, e.g. main..HEAD
  --diff-file <PATH>    a unified diff file instead of --repo/--diff
  --recordings <DIR>    directory of recordings to collect from
  --output, -o <DIR>    output directory for the dataset (required)
  --preset <NAME>       default | minimal | comprehensive
  --progress            emit JSON Lines progress events on stderr

`collect` writes the dataset chunks and a `review.json` beside them, so
`ct review <the same DIR>` opens what it just produced.

--- stderr
--- end

 host, and `mcr` also pins it. Linux additionally accepts

 <arg>` arg.\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--store-trace-folder-for-pid\e[0m
 sets a pid, if we should store the resulting trace folder
 in a special tmp file, grouping info for a certain
 originating codetracer pid. 0 means 'do not store in such
 a file' [=0].\e[0m

 it.\e[0m

 Record graphics API calls for visual replay (MCR backend
 only). [=false].\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--server                \e[0m
 Record a long-lived server. The recording stays readable
 while it runs, so `ct replay -t <folder>` in another
 terminal shows requests arriving live; stop it with
 Ctrl-C. [=false].\e[0m
     \e[34m\e[1m--portable              \e[0m
 Make the trace replayable on another machine, or later
 after its files change: the MCR backend bundles every file
 the program mapped, their debug symbols and the platform
 description into the trace, and the rr backend packs the
 files the trace mapped into it. A backend that cannot do
 this yet refuses the flag. Also honors
 CODETRACER_PORTABLE=on|off; --upload implies it, and warns
 where it is not implemented. [=false].\e[0m

### 79: ct-completions "bash"
argv: ct "ct-completions" "bash"
env: (none)
exit: 0
--- stdout
# bash completion script for the ct launcher
# Generated by `codetracer ct-completions bash`.
# Source this file (e.g. add to /etc/bash_completion.d/ct) or
# evaluate inline: `eval "$(codetracer ct-completions bash)"`.
#
# At runtime, _ct_completions calls `codetracer ct-complete ...`
# directly -- bypassing the `ct` launcher exec hop (spec §2.7).
_ct_completions() {
  local IFS=$'\n'
  local cur_words=("${COMP_WORDS[@]:1}")
  COMPREPLY=($(codetracer ct-complete "${cur_words[@]}"))
}
complete -F _ct_completions ct
--- stderr
--- end

### 80: ct-completions "zsh"
argv: ct "ct-completions" "zsh"
env: (none)
exit: 0
--- stdout
#compdef ct
# zsh completion script for the ct launcher
# Generated by `codetracer ct-completions zsh`.
# Save under a directory in $fpath (e.g. /usr/share/zsh/site-functions/_ct)
# or source inline: `source <(codetracer ct-completions zsh)`.
#
# At runtime, _ct calls `codetracer ct-complete ...` directly --
# bypassing the `ct` launcher exec hop (spec §2.7).
_ct() {
  local -a candidates
  local cur_word="${words[CURRENT]}"
  local -a partial
  partial=("${(@)words[2,CURRENT]}")
  candidates=("${(@f)$(codetracer ct-complete "${partial[@]}")}")
  compadd -a candidates
}
compdef _ct ct
--- stderr
--- end

### 81: ct-completions "fish"
argv: ct "ct-completions" "fish"
env: (none)
exit: 0
--- stdout
# fish completion script for the ct launcher
# Generated by `codetracer ct-completions fish`.
# Save under ~/.config/fish/completions/ct.fish or source it.
#
# At runtime, the completion calls `codetracer ct-complete ...`
# directly -- bypassing the `ct` launcher exec hop (spec §2.7).
function __ct_complete
  set -l tokens (commandline -opc)
  set -l current (commandline -ct)
  set -l partial $tokens[2..-1] $current
  codetracer ct-complete $partial
end
complete -c ct -f -a '(__ct_complete)'
--- stderr
--- end
